crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:363:    /// Return the matcher that should be used for searching using the engine
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:364:    /// choice made by the user.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:365:    ///
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:366:    /// If there was a problem building the matcher (e.g., a syntax error),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:367:    /// then this returns an error.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:368:    pub(crate) fn matcher(&self) -> anyhow::Result<PatternMatcher> {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:369:        match self.engine {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:370:            EngineChoice::Default => match self.matcher_rust() {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:371:                Ok(m) => Ok(m),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:372:                Err(err) => {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:373:                    anyhow::bail!(suggest_other_engine(err.to_string()));
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:374:                }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:375:            },
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:376:            EngineChoice::PCRE2 => Ok(self.matcher_pcre2()?),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:377:            EngineChoice::Auto => {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:378:                let rust_err = match self.matcher_rust() {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:379:                    Ok(m) => return Ok(m),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:380:                    Err(err) => err,
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:381:                };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:382:                log::debug!(
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:383:                    "error building Rust regex in hybrid mode:\n{rust_err}",
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:384:                );
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:386:                let pcre_err = match self.matcher_pcre2() {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:387:                    Ok(m) => return Ok(m),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:388:                    Err(err) => err,
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:389:                };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:390:                let divider = "~".repeat(79);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:391:                anyhow::bail!(
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:392:                    "regex could not be compiled with either the default \
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:393:                     regex engine or with PCRE2.\n\n\
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:394:                     default regex engine error:\n\
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:395:                     {divider}\n\
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:396:                     {rust_err}\n\
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:397:                     {divider}\n\n\
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:398:                     PCRE2 regex engine error:\n{pcre_err}",
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:399:                );
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:400:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:401:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:402:    }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:404:    /// Build a matcher using PCRE2.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:405:    ///
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:406:    /// If there was a problem building the matcher (such as a regex syntax
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:407:    /// error), then an error is returned.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:408:    ///
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:409:    /// If the `pcre2` feature is not enabled then this always returns an
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:410:    /// error.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:411:    fn matcher_pcre2(&self) -> anyhow::Result<PatternMatcher> {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:412:        #[cfg(feature = "pcre2")]
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:413:        {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:414:            let mut builder = grep::pcre2::RegexMatcherBuilder::new();
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:415:            builder.multi_line(true).fixed_strings(self.fixed_strings);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:416:            match self.case {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:417:                CaseMode::Sensitive => builder.caseless(false),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:418:                CaseMode::Insensitive => builder.caseless(true),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:419:                CaseMode::Smart => builder.case_smart(true),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:420:            };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:421:            if let Some(ref boundary) = self.boundary {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:422:                match *boundary {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:423:                    BoundaryMode::Line => builder.whole_line(true),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:424:                    BoundaryMode::Word => builder.word(true),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:425:                };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:426:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:427:            // For whatever reason, the JIT craps out during regex compilation with
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:428:            // a "no more memory" error on 32 bit systems. So don't use it there.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:429:            if cfg!(target_pointer_width = "64") {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:430:                builder
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:431:                    .jit_if_available(true)
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:432:                    // The PCRE2 docs say that 32KB is the default, and that 1MB
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:433:                    // should be big enough for anything. But let's crank it to
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:434:                    // 10MB.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:435:                    .max_jit_stack_size(Some(10 * (1 << 20)));
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:436:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:437:            if !self.no_unicode {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:438:                builder.utf(true).ucp(true);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:439:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:440:            if self.multiline {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:441:                builder.dotall(self.multiline_dotall);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:442:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:443:            if self.crlf {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:444:                builder.crlf(true);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:445:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:446:            let m = builder.build_many(&self.patterns.patterns)?;
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:447:            Ok(PatternMatcher::PCRE2(m))
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:448:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:449:        #[cfg(not(feature = "pcre2"))]
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:450:        {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:451:            Err(anyhow::anyhow!(
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:452:                "PCRE2 is not available in this build of ripgrep"
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:453:            ))
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:454:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:455:    }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:457:    /// Build a matcher using Rust's regex engine.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:458:    ///
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:459:    /// If there was a problem building the matcher (such as a regex syntax
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:460:    /// error), then an error is returned.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:461:    fn matcher_rust(&self) -> anyhow::Result<PatternMatcher> {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:462:        let mut builder = grep::regex::RegexMatcherBuilder::new();
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:463:        builder
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:464:            .multi_line(true)
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:465:            .unicode(!self.no_unicode)
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:466:            .octal(false)
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:467:            .fixed_strings(self.fixed_strings);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:468:        match self.case {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:469:            CaseMode::Sensitive => builder.case_insensitive(false),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:470:            CaseMode::Insensitive => builder.case_insensitive(true),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:471:            CaseMode::Smart => builder.case_smart(true),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:472:        };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:473:        if let Some(ref boundary) = self.boundary {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:474:            match *boundary {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:475:                BoundaryMode::Line => builder.whole_line(true),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:476:                BoundaryMode::Word => builder.word(true),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:477:            };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:478:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:479:        if self.multiline {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:480:            builder.dot_matches_new_line(self.multiline_dotall);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:481:            if self.crlf {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:482:                builder.crlf(true).line_terminator(None);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:483:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:484:        } else {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:485:            builder.line_terminator(Some(b'\n')).dot_matches_new_line(false);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:486:            if self.crlf {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:487:                builder.crlf(true);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:488:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:489:            // We don't need to set this in multiline mode since multiline
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:490:            // matchers don't use optimizations related to line terminators.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:491:            // Moreover, a multiline regex used with --null-data should
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:492:            // be allowed to match NUL bytes explicitly, which this would
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:493:            // otherwise forbid.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:494:            if self.null_data {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:495:                builder.line_terminator(Some(b'\x00'));
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:496:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:497:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:498:        if let Some(limit) = self.regex_size_limit {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:499:            builder.size_limit(limit);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:500:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:501:        if let Some(limit) = self.dfa_size_limit {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:502:            builder.dfa_size_limit(limit);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:503:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:504:        if !self.binary.is_none() {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:505:            builder.ban_byte(Some(b'\x00'));
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:506:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:507:        let m = match builder.build_many(&self.patterns.patterns) {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:508:            Ok(m) => m,
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:509:            Err(err) => {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:510:                anyhow::bail!(suggest_text(suggest_multiline(err.to_string())))
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:511:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:512:        };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:513:        Ok(PatternMatcher::RustRegex(m))
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:514:    }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:516:    /// Returns true if some non-zero number of matches is believed to be
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:517:    /// possible.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:518:    ///
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:519:    /// When this returns false, it is impossible for ripgrep to ever report
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:520:    /// a match.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:521:    pub(crate) fn matches_possible(&self) -> bool {

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:750:    /// Given an iterator of haystacks, sort them if necessary.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:751:    ///
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:752:    /// When sorting is necessary, this will collect the entire iterator into
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:753:    /// memory, sort them and then return a new iterator. When sorting is not
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:754:    /// necessary, then the iterator given is returned as is without collecting
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:755:    /// it into memory.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:756:    ///
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:757:    /// Once special case is when sorting by path in ascending order has been
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:758:    /// requested. In this case, the iterator given is returned as is without
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:759:    /// any additional sorting. This is done because `walk_builder()` will sort
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:760:    /// the iterator it yields during directory traversal, so no additional
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:761:    /// sorting is needed.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:762:    pub(crate) fn sort<'a, I>(
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:763:        &self,
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:764:        haystacks: I,
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:765:    ) -> Box<dyn Iterator<Item = Haystack> + 'a>
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:766:    where
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:767:        I: Iterator<Item = Haystack> + 'a,
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:768:    {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:769:        use std::{cmp::Ordering, fs::Metadata, io, time::SystemTime};
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:771:        fn attach_timestamps(
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:772:            haystacks: impl Iterator<Item = Haystack>,
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:773:            get: impl Fn(&Metadata) -> io::Result<SystemTime>,
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:774:        ) -> impl Iterator<Item = (Haystack, Option<SystemTime>)> {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:775:            haystacks.map(move |s| {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:776:                let time = s.path().metadata().and_then(|m| get(&m)).ok();
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:777:                (s, time)
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:778:            })
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:779:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:781:        let Some(ref sort) = self.sort else { return Box::new(haystacks) };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:782:        let mut with_timestamps: Vec<_> = match sort.kind {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:783:            SortModeKind::Path if !sort.reverse => return Box::new(haystacks),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:784:            SortModeKind::Path => {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:785:                let mut haystacks = haystacks.collect::<Vec<Haystack>>();
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:786:                haystacks.sort_by(|ref h1, ref h2| {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:787:                    h1.path().cmp(h2.path()).reverse()
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:788:                });
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:789:                return Box::new(haystacks.into_iter());
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:790:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:791:            SortModeKind::LastModified => {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:792:                attach_timestamps(haystacks, |md| md.modified()).collect()
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:793:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:794:            SortModeKind::LastAccessed => {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:795:                attach_timestamps(haystacks, |md| md.accessed()).collect()
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:796:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:797:            SortModeKind::Created => {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:798:                attach_timestamps(haystacks, |md| md.created()).collect()
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:799:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:800:        };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:801:        with_timestamps.sort_by(|(_, t1), (_, t2)| {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:802:            let ordering = match (*t1, *t2) {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:803:                // Both have metadata, do the obvious thing.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:804:                (Some(t1), Some(t2)) => t1.cmp(&t2),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:805:                // Things that error should appear later (when ascending).
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:806:                (Some(_), None) => Ordering::Less,
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:807:                // Things that error should appear later (when ascending).
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:808:                (None, Some(_)) => Ordering::Greater,
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:809:                // When both error, we can't distinguish, so treat as equal.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:810:                (None, None) => Ordering::Equal,
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:811:            };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:812:            if sort.reverse { ordering.reverse() } else { ordering }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:813:        });
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:814:        Box::new(with_timestamps.into_iter().map(|(s, _)| s))
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:815:    }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:817:    /// Returns a stats object if the user requested that ripgrep keep track

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:819:    ///

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:826:    /// Returns a color-enabled writer for stdout.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:827:    ///
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:828:    /// The writer returned is also configured to do either line or block
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:829:    /// buffering, based on either explicit configuration from the user via CLI
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:830:    /// flags, or automatically based on whether stdout is connected to a tty.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:831:    pub(crate) fn stdout(&self) -> grep::cli::StandardStream {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:832:        let color = self.color.to_termcolor();
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:833:        match self.buffer {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:834:            BufferMode::Auto => {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:835:                if self.is_terminal_stdout {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:836:                    grep::cli::stdout_buffered_line(color)
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:837:                } else {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:838:                    grep::cli::stdout_buffered_block(color)
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:839:                }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:840:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:841:            BufferMode::Line => grep::cli::stdout_buffered_line(color),

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:976:    ///
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:977:    /// If the invocation implies that the first positional argument is a
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:978:    /// pattern (the common case), then the first positional argument is

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:980:    fn from_low_args(

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:984:        // The first positional is only a pattern when ripgrep is instructed to
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:985:        // search and neither -e/--regexp nor -f/--file is given. Basically,

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:987:        // given in some other way.

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:993:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:994:        // If we got nothing from -e/--regexp and -f/--file, then the first
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:995:        // positional is a pattern.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:996:        if low.patterns.is_empty() {

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1014:        // extra cost. It is lamentable that we pay the extra cost here to
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1015:        // de-duplicate for a likely uncommon case, but I've seen this have a
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1016:        // big impact on real world data.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1017:        let mut seen = HashSet::new();
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1018:        let mut patterns = Vec::with_capacity(low.patterns.len());
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1019:        let mut add = |pat: String| {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1020:            if !seen.contains(&pat) {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1021:                seen.insert(pat.clone());
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1022:                patterns.push(pat);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1023:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1024:        };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1025:        for source in low.patterns.drain(..) {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1026:            match source {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1027:                PatternSource::Regexp(pat) => add(pat),
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1028:                PatternSource::File(path) => {

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1030:                        anyhow::ensure!(
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1031:                            !state.stdin_consumed,

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1036:                            add(pat);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1037:                        }

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1039:                    } else {

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1041:                            add(pat);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1042:                        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1043:                    }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1044:                }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1045:            }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1046:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1047:        Ok(Patterns { patterns })
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1048:    }

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1107:        // lot of "exec process" APIs will open a stdin pipe even though stdin

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1142:/// The "binary detection" configuration that ripgrep should use.
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1143:///
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1144:/// ripgrep actually uses two different binary detection heuristics depending
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1145:/// on whether a file is explicitly being searched (e.g., via a CLI argument)

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1147:/// former can never use a heuristic that lets it "quit" searching before

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1316:/// Currently, this is only used in the hyperlink format.

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1325:fn hostname(bin: Option<&Path>) -> Option<String> {
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1326:    let Some(bin) = bin else { return platform_hostname() };

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1334:            return platform_hostname();
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1335:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1336:    };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1337:    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new(&bin);
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1338:    cmd.stdin(std::process::Stdio::null());

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1341:        Err(err) => {

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1345:            );
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1346:            return platform_hostname();
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1347:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1348:    };

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1355:            );
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1356:            return platform_hostname();
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1357:        }
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1358:    };
crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1359:    let hostname = out.trim();

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1365:        );

crates/core/flags/hiargs.rs:1368:    Some(hostname.to_string())

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1:/*!
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2:Defines all of the flags available in ripgrep.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4:Each flag corresponds to a unit struct with a corresponding implementation
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:5:of `Flag`. Note that each implementation of `Flag` might actually have many
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6:possible manifestations of the same "flag." That is, each implementation of
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:7:`Flag` can have the following flags available to an end user of ripgrep:
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:9:* The long flag name.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:10:* An optional short flag name.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:11:* An optional negated long flag name.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:12:* An arbitrarily long list of aliases.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:14:The idea is that even though there are multiple flags that a user can type,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:15:one implementation of `Flag` corresponds to a single _logical_ flag inside of
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:16:ripgrep. For example, `-E`, `--encoding` and `--no-encoding` all manipulate the
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:17:same encoding state in ripgrep.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:18:*/

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:263:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:264:When this flag is used, ripgrep will dynamically choose between supported regex
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:265:engines depending on the features used in a pattern. When ripgrep chooses a
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:266:regex engine, it applies that choice for every regex provided to ripgrep (e.g.,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:267:via multiple \flag{regexp} or \flag{file} flags).
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:268:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:269:As an example of how this flag might behave, ripgrep will attempt to use
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:270:its default finite automata based regex engine whenever the pattern can be
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:271:successfully compiled with that regex engine. If PCRE2 is enabled and if the
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:272:pattern given could not be compiled with the default regex engine, then PCRE2
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:273:will be automatically used for searching. If PCRE2 isn't available, then this
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:274:flag has no effect because there is only one regex engine to choose from.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:275:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:276:In the future, ripgrep may adjust its heuristics for how it decides which
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:277:regex engine to use. In general, the heuristics will be limited to a static
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:278:analysis of the patterns, and not to any specific runtime behavior observed
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:279:while searching files.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:280:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:281:The primary downside of using this flag is that it may not always be obvious
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:282:which regex engine ripgrep uses, and thus, the match semantics or performance
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:283:profile of ripgrep may subtly and unexpectedly change. However, in many cases,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:284:all regex engines will agree on what constitutes a match and it can be nice
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:285:to transparently support more advanced regex features like look-around and
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:286:backreferences without explicitly needing to enable them.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:287:"

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:445:    fn doc_long(&self) -> &'static str {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:446:        r"
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:447:Enabling this flag will cause ripgrep to search binary files. By default,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:448:ripgrep attempts to automatically skip binary files in order to improve the
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:449:relevance of results and make the search faster.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:450:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:451:Binary files are heuristically detected based on whether they contain a
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:452:\fBNUL\fP byte or not. By default (without this flag set), once a \fBNUL\fP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:453:byte is seen, ripgrep will stop searching the file. Usually, \fBNUL\fP bytes
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:454:occur in the beginning of most binary files. If a \fBNUL\fP byte occurs after
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:455:a match, then ripgrep will not print the match, stop searching that file, and
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:456:emit a warning that some matches are being suppressed.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:457:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:458:In contrast, when this flag is provided, ripgrep will continue searching a
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:459:file even if a \fBNUL\fP byte is found. In particular, if a \fBNUL\fP byte is
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:460:found then ripgrep will continue searching until either a match is found or
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:461:the end of the file is reached, whichever comes sooner. If a match is found,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:462:then ripgrep will stop and print a warning saying that the search stopped
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:463:prematurely.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:464:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:465:If you want ripgrep to search a file without any special \fBNUL\fP byte
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:466:handling at all (and potentially print binary data to stdout), then you should
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:467:use the \flag{text} flag.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:468:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:469:The \flag{binary} flag is a flag for controlling ripgrep's automatic filtering
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:470:mechanism. As such, it does not need to be used when searching a file
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:471:explicitly or when searching stdin. That is, it is only applicable when
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:472:recursively searching a directory.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:473:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:474:When the \flag{unrestricted} flag is provided for a third time, then this flag
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:475:is automatically enabled.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:476:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:477:This flag overrides the \flag{text} flag.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:478:"
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:479:    }

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:834:This flag specifies color settings for use in the output. This flag may be
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:835:provided multiple times. Settings are applied iteratively. Pre-existing color
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:836:labels are limited to one of eight choices: \fBred\fP, \fBblue\fP, \fBgreen\fP,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:837:\fBcyan\fP, \fBmagenta\fP, \fByellow\fP, \fBwhite\fP and \fBblack\fP. Styles

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:839:\fBnounderline\fP, \fBunderline\fP, \fBnoitalic\fP or \fBitalic\fP.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:840:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:841:The format of the flag is
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:842:\fB{\fP\fItype\fP\fB}:{\fP\fIattribute\fP\fB}:{\fP\fIvalue\fP\fB}\fP.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:843:\fItype\fP should be one of \fBpath\fP, \fBline\fP, \fBcolumn\fP,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:844:\fBhighlight\fP or \fBmatch\fP. \fIattribute\fP can be \fBfg\fP, \fBbg\fP or
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:845:\fBstyle\fP. \fIvalue\fP is either a color (for \fBfg\fP and \fBbg\fP) or a
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:846:text style. A special format, \fB{\fP\fItype\fP\fB}:none\fP, will clear all
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:847:color settings for \fItype\fP.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:848:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:849:For example, the following command will change the match color to magenta and
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:850:the background color for line numbers to yellow:

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:855:.sp

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:869:.IB x , x , x

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1189:    use bstr::BString;
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1191:    use crate::flags::lowargs::ContextSeparator as Separator;

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1205:        "--context-separator",

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1208:    ])
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1209:    .unwrap();
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1210:    assert_eq!(None, getbytes(args.context_separator));
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1212:    let args = parse_low_raw([
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1213:        "--no-context-separator",
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1214:        "--context-separator",
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1215:        "XYZ",
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1216:    ])
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1217:    .unwrap();
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1218:    assert_eq!(Some(BString::from("XYZ")), getbytes(args.context_separator));
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1220:    // This checks that invalid UTF-8 can be used. This case isn't too tricky
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1221:    // to handle, because it passes the invalid UTF-8 as an escape sequence
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1222:    // that is itself valid UTF-8. It doesn't become invalid UTF-8 until after
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1223:    // the argument is parsed and then unescaped.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1224:    let args = parse_low_raw(["--context-separator", r"\xFF"]).unwrap();
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1225:    assert_eq!(Some(BString::from(b"\xFF")), getbytes(args.context_separator));
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1227:    // In this case, we specifically try to pass an invalid UTF-8 argument to
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1228:    // the flag. In theory we might be able to support this, but because we do
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1229:    // unescaping and because unescaping wants valid UTF-8, we do a UTF-8 check
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1230:    // on the value. Since we pass invalid UTF-8, it fails. This demonstrates
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1231:    // that the only way to use an invalid UTF-8 separator is by specifying an
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1232:    // escape sequence that is itself valid UTF-8.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1233:    #[cfg(unix)]

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1235:        use std::{ffi::OsStr, os::unix::ffi::OsStrExt};

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1238:            OsStr::from_bytes(b"--context-separator"),
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1239:            OsStr::from_bytes(&[0xFF]),
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1240:        ]);
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1241:        assert!(result.is_err(), "{result:?}");

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1711:Specify which regular expression engine to use. When you choose a regex engine,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1712:it applies that choice for every regex provided to ripgrep (e.g., via multiple
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1713:\flag{regexp} or \flag{file} flags).

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1715:Accepted values are \fBdefault\fP, \fBpcre2\fP, or \fBauto\fP.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1716:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1717:The default value is \fBdefault\fP, which is usually the fastest and should be
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1718:good for most use cases. The \fBpcre2\fP engine is generally useful when you
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1719:want to use features such as look-around or backreferences. \fBauto\fP will
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1720:dynamically choose between supported regex engines depending on the features

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:1722:.sp

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2909:    }
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2910:    fn doc_category(&self) -> Category {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2911:        Category::Output
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2912:    }
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2913:    fn doc_short(&self) -> &'static str {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2914:        r"Set the format of hyperlinks."
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2915:    }
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2916:    fn doc_long(&self) -> &'static str {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2917:        static DOC: LazyLock<String> = LazyLock::new(|| {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2918:            let mut doc = String::new();
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2919:            doc.push_str(
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2920:                r#"
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2921:Set the format of hyperlinks to use when printing results. Hyperlinks make
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2922:certain elements of ripgrep's output, such as file paths, clickable. This
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2923:generally only works in terminal emulators that support OSC-8 hyperlinks. For
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2924:example, the format \fBfile://{host}{path}\fP will emit an RFC 8089 hyperlink.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2925:To see the format that ripgrep is using, pass the \flag{debug} flag.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2926:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2927:Alternatively, a format string may correspond to one of the following aliases:
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2928:"#,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2929:            );
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2931:            let mut aliases = grep::printer::hyperlink_aliases();
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2932:            aliases.sort_by_key(|alias| {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2933:                alias.display_priority().unwrap_or(i16::MAX)
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2934:            });
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2935:            for (i, alias) in aliases.iter().enumerate() {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2936:                doc.push_str(r"\fB");
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2937:                doc.push_str(alias.name());
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2938:                doc.push_str(r"\fP");
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2939:                doc.push_str(if i < aliases.len() - 1 { ", " } else { "." });
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2940:            }
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2941:            doc.push_str(
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2942:                r#"
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2943:The alias will be replaced with a format string that is intended to work for
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2944:the corresponding application.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2945:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2946:The following variables are available in the format string:
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2947:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2948:.TP 12
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2949:\fB{path}\fP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2950:Required. This is replaced with a path to a matching file. The path is
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2951:guaranteed to be absolute and percent encoded such that it is valid to put into
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2952:a URI. Note that a path is guaranteed to start with a /.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2953:.TP 12
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2954:\fB{host}\fP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2955:Optional. This is replaced with your system's hostname. On Unix, this
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2956:corresponds to calling \fBgethostname\fP. On Windows, this corresponds to
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2957:calling \fBGetComputerNameExW\fP to fetch the system's "physical DNS hostname."
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2958:Alternatively, if \flag{hostname-bin} was provided, then the hostname returned
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2959:from the output of that program will be returned. If no hostname could be
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2960:found, then this variable is replaced with the empty string.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2961:.TP 12
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2962:\fB{line}\fP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2963:Optional. If appropriate, this is replaced with the line number of a match. If
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2964:no line number is available (for example, if \fB\-\-no\-line\-number\fP was
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2965:given), then it is automatically replaced with the value 1.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2966:.TP 12
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2967:\fB{column}\fP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2968:Optional, but requires the presence of \fB{line}\fP. If appropriate, this is
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2969:replaced with the column number of a match. If no column number is available
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2970:(for example, if \fB\-\-no\-column\fP was given), then it is automatically
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2971:replaced with the value 1.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2972:.TP 12
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2973:\fB{wslprefix}\fP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2974:Optional. This is a special value that is set to
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2975:\fBwsl$/\fP\fIWSL_DISTRO_NAME\fP, where \fIWSL_DISTRO_NAME\fP corresponds to
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2976:the value of the equivalent environment variable. If the system is not Unix
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2977:or if the \fIWSL_DISTRO_NAME\fP environment variable is not set, then this is
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2978:replaced with the empty string.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2979:.PP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2980:A format string may be empty. An empty format string is equivalent to the
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2981:\fBnone\fP alias. In this case, hyperlinks will be disabled.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2982:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2983:At present, ripgrep does not enable hyperlinks by default. Users must opt into
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2984:them. If you aren't sure what format to use, try \fBdefault\fP.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2985:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2986:Like colors, when ripgrep detects that stdout is not connected to a tty, then
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2987:hyperlinks are automatically disabled, regardless of the value of this flag.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2988:Users can pass \fB\-\-color=always\fP to forcefully emit hyperlinks.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2989:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2990:Note that hyperlinks are only written when a path is also in the output
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2991:and colors are enabled. To write hyperlinks without colors, you'll need to
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2992:configure ripgrep to not colorize anything without actually disabling all ANSI
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2993:escape codes completely:
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2994:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2995:.EX
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2996:    \-\-colors 'path:none' \\
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2997:    \-\-colors 'line:none' \\
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2998:    \-\-colors 'column:none' \\
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:2999:    \-\-colors 'match:none'
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3000:.EE
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3001:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3002:ripgrep works this way because it treats the \flag{color} flag as a proxy for
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3003:whether ANSI escape codes should be used at all. This means that environment
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3004:variables like \fBNO_COLOR=1\fP and \fBTERM=dumb\fP not only disable colors,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3005:but hyperlinks as well. Similarly, colors and hyperlinks are disabled when
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3006:ripgrep is not writing to a tty. (Unless one forces the issue by setting
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3007:\fB\-\-color=always\fP.)
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3008:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3009:If you're searching a file directly, for example:
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3010:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3011:.EX
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3012:    rg foo path/to/file
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3013:.EE
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3014:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3015:then hyperlinks will not be emitted since the path given does not appear
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3016:in the output. To make the path appear, and thus also a hyperlink, use the
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3017:\flag{with-filename} flag.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3018:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3019:For more information on hyperlinks in terminal emulators, see:

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3022:            );

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3025:        &DOC
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3026:    }
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3028:    fn doc_choices(&self) -> &'static [&'static str] {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3029:        static CHOICES: LazyLock<Vec<String>> = LazyLock::new(|| {

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3454:    fn doc_long(&self) -> &'static str {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3455:        r"
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3456:Enable printing results in a JSON Lines format.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3457:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3458:When this flag is provided, ripgrep will emit a sequence of messages, each
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3459:encoded as a JSON object, where there are five different message types:
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3460:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3461:.TP 12
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3462:\fBbegin\fP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3463:A message that indicates a file is being searched and contains at least one
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3464:match.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3465:.TP 12
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3466:\fBend\fP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3467:A message the indicates a file is done being searched. This message also
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3468:include summary statistics about the search for a particular file.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3469:.TP 12
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3470:\fBmatch\fP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3471:A message that indicates a match was found. This includes the text and offsets
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3472:of the match.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3473:.TP 12
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3474:\fBcontext\fP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3475:A message that indicates a contextual line was found. This includes the text of
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3476:the line, along with any match information if the search was inverted.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3477:.TP 12
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3478:\fBsummary\fP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3479:The final message emitted by ripgrep that contains summary statistics about the
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3480:search across all files.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3481:.PP
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3482:Since file paths or the contents of files are not guaranteed to be valid
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3483:UTF-8 and JSON itself must be representable by a Unicode encoding, ripgrep
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3484:will emit all data elements as objects with one of two keys: \fBtext\fP or
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3485:\fBbytes\fP. \fBtext\fP is a normal JSON string when the data is valid UTF-8
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3486:while \fBbytes\fP is the base64 encoded contents of the data.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3487:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3488:The JSON Lines format is only supported for showing search results. It cannot
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3489:be used with other flags that emit other types of output, such as \flag{files},
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3490:\flag{files-with-matches}, \flag{files-without-match}, \flag{count} or
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3491:\flag{count-matches}. ripgrep will report an error if any of the aforementioned
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3492:flags are used in concert with \flag{json}.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3493:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3494:Other flags that control aspects of the standard output such as
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3495:\flag{only-matching}, \flag{heading}, \flag{replace}, \flag{max-columns}, etc.,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3496:have no effect when \flag{json} is set. However, enabling JSON output will
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3497:always implicitly and unconditionally enable \flag{stats}.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3498:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3499:A more complete description of the JSON format used can be found here:
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3500:\fIhttps://docs.rs/grep-printer/*/grep_printer/struct.JSON.html\fP.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3501:"
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3502:    }
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3504:    fn update(&self, v: FlagValue, args: &mut LowArgs) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3505:        if v.unwrap_switch() {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3506:            args.mode.update(Mode::Search(SearchMode::JSON));

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:3512:        Ok(())

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4144:    fn doc_short(&self) -> &'static str {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4145:        r"Enable searching across multiple lines."
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4146:    }
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4147:    fn doc_long(&self) -> &'static str {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4148:        r#"
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4149:This flag enables searching across multiple lines.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4150:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4151:When multiline mode is enabled, ripgrep will lift the restriction that a
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4152:match cannot include a line terminator. For example, when multiline mode
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4153:is not enabled (the default), then the regex \fB\\p{any}\fP will match any
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4154:Unicode codepoint other than \fB\\n\fP. Similarly, the regex \fB\\n\fP is
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4155:explicitly forbidden, and if you try to use it, ripgrep will return an error.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4156:However, when multiline mode is enabled, \fB\\p{any}\fP will match any Unicode
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4157:codepoint, including \fB\\n\fP, and regexes like \fB\\n\fP are permitted.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4158:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4159:An important caveat is that multiline mode does not change the match semantics
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4160:of \fB.\fP. Namely, in most regex matchers, a \fB.\fP will by default match any
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4161:character other than \fB\\n\fP, and this is true in ripgrep as well. In order
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4162:to make \fB.\fP match \fB\\n\fP, you must enable the "dot all" flag inside the
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4163:regex. For example, both \fB(?s).\fP and \fB(?s:.)\fP have the same semantics,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4164:where \fB.\fP will match any character, including \fB\\n\fP. Alternatively, the
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4165:\flag{multiline-dotall} flag may be passed to make the "dot all" behavior the
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4166:default. This flag only applies when multiline search is enabled.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4167:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4168:There is no limit on the number of the lines that a single match can span.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4169:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4170:\fBWARNING\fP: Because of how the underlying regex engine works, multiline
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4171:searches may be slower than normal line-oriented searches, and they may also
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4172:use more memory. In particular, when multiline mode is enabled, ripgrep
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4173:requires that each file it searches is laid out contiguously in memory (either
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4174:by reading it onto the heap or by memory-mapping it). Things that cannot be
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4175:memory-mapped (such as \fBstdin\fP) will be consumed until EOF before searching
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4176:can begin. In general, ripgrep will only do these things when necessary.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4177:Specifically, if the \flag{multiline} flag is provided but the regex does
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4178:not contain patterns that would match \fB\\n\fP characters, then ripgrep
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4179:will automatically avoid reading each file into memory before searching it.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4180:Nevertheless, if you only care about matches spanning at most one line, then it
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4181:is always better to disable multiline mode.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4182:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4183:This overrides the \flag{stop-on-nonmatch} flag.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4184:"#
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4185:    }
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4187:    fn update(&self, v: FlagValue, args: &mut LowArgs) -> anyhow::Result<()> {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4188:        args.multiline = v.unwrap_switch();
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4189:        if args.multiline {
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4190:            args.stop_on_nonmatch = false;
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4191:        }
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4192:        Ok(())
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4193:    }
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4194:}

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4908:By default, ripgrep will enable "Unicode mode" in all of its regexes. This has

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4910:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:4911:.IP \(bu 3n

crates/core/flags/defs.rs:5996:Replaces every match with the text given when printing results. Neither this
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:5997:flag nor any other ripgrep flag will modify your files.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:5998:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:5999:Capture group indices (e.g., \fB$\fP\fI5\fP) and names (e.g., \fB$\fP\fIfoo\fP)
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6000:are supported in the replacement string. Capture group indices are numbered
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6001:based on the position of the opening parenthesis of the group, where the
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6002:leftmost such group is \fB$\fP\fI1\fP. The special \fB$\fP\fI0\fP group
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6003:corresponds to the entire match.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6004:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6005:The name of a group is formed by taking the longest string of letters, numbers
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6006:and underscores (i.e. \fB[_0-9A-Za-z]\fP) after the \fB$\fP. For example,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6007:\fB$\fP\fI1a\fP will be replaced with the group named \fI1a\fP, not the
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6008:group at index \fI1\fP. If the group's name contains characters that aren't
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6009:letters, numbers or underscores, or you want to immediately follow the group
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6010:with another string, the name should be put inside braces. For example,
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6011:\fB${\fP\fI1\fP\fB}\fP\fIa\fP will take the content of the group at index
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6012:\fI1\fP and append \fIa\fP to the end of it.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6013:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6014:If an index or name does not refer to a valid capture group, it will be
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6015:replaced with an empty string.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6016:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6017:In shells such as Bash and zsh, you should wrap the pattern in single quotes
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6018:instead of double quotes. Otherwise, capture group indices will be replaced by
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6019:expanded shell variables which will most likely be empty.
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6020:.sp
crates/core/flags/defs.rs:6021:To write a literal \fB$\fP, use \fB$$\fP.
